# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-14_TotalExtraction_18360318.pdf
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Wesley D. Bay, P.E., Deputy City Engineer 
 
DATE: 
February 14, 2022 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement for Professional Land Surveying Services. 
 
 
Please find the attached Professional Service Agreement between the City and Steil Surveying 
Services, LLC. for surveying services for the City of Cheyenne. 
  
Engineering Staff evaluates fee proposals for engineering services based on time and material 
estimates for the specific project.  Those costs are readily tracked and verified during 
design/review operations and the City only pays for the actual time expended. We believe this 
agreement will provide the required services to complete the surveying related tasks necessary for 
the review of City and developer projects. 
 
City funding for these services will come from 2019 – 2022 1% sales tax. The total professional 
services will be a cost not-to-exceed $25,000.00. We respectfully request your approval of this 
professional services agreement. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

1 
 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
Professional Land Surveying Services 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _______ day of ________________, 2022, by 
and between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Steil Surveying Services, LLC., 1102 West 19th Street, Cheyenne, WY 82001 
(“Consultant”). 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by and 
between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by reference 
and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by January 30, 2023, as stated 
in Exhibit A. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant 
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of Twenty-
Five Thousand Dollars ($25,000.00). 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the Consultant, 
the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not meeting 
such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 2:45 pm, Feb 08, 2022

2 
 
 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of Cheyenne 
has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and 
drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers and other consultants under 
contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are required to 
comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug 
and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the Consultant while 
performing the duties and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the responsibility of the 
Consultant to familiarize themselves with the requirements of this policy and to inform all 
their employees and sub-consultants of the requirements and ensure their compliance. If 
the Consultant, their employees or sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the 
Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result 
the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any person, 
destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or sub-
consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges that it 
may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 per 
occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 

3 
 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided 
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the 
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a 
new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be 
in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services under 
this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., 
b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance 
written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall provide the 
City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) 
which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than five (5) years 
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this 
Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above-
stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All policies 
required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  It shall 
be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the city risk 
manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has 
resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance 
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach 
of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  

4 
 
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not 
affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written 
statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, and 
work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of any 
payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that 
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this 
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to 
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of 
any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts 
and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
 

5 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from the date of execution to January 30, 2023.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon 
receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant 
for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. 
Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the 
City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of default 
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to 
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its 
own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for 
the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 

6 
 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts 
and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to 
examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; 
and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne and 
shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of war, 
acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental regulation or 
advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health Organization, the 
Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health agencies (including, 
but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), 
curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence beyond the control of 
the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar 
ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance required by this Agreement. 
The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without liability, damages, fees, or 
penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more 
of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing that 
he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of seven (7) pages, and Exhibit A consisting of two (2) pages, 
contain the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, 
written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter.  
 

7 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties have hereunto set their hands and seals the day and year 
first above written.

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Professional Land Surveying Services 
 
 
 
1
Exhibit A – Scope of Work 
Overview 
 
On-call surveying services will be to perform surveying activities for the City Engineering Department. 
The nature of the work will be on an on-call basis and will generally include but not necessarily be 
limited to: responding to surveying issues; performing the technical review of plans, reports, and 
specifications; reviewing development projects; reviewing survey related ordinances, regulations, and 
policies; and providing complex land surveying tasks. 
 
On-call surveying services will require registration as a Professional Land Surveyor in the State of Wyoming 
with a minimum of 5 years of land surveying experience. 
 
Description 
 
More specifically, Professional Land Surveying services will entail: 
• 
Serve as a technical advisor to the City Engineer on survey related matters. 
  
• 
Review and process tract maps, parcel maps, lot line adjustments, grants of easement, and street 
rights-of-way for recondition. 
 
• 
Verify fulfillment of conditions of approval for new subdivisions; perform on-site inspections to 
verify survey monuments related to parcel maps and lot line adjustments. 
 
• 
Prepare legal descriptions and exhibits. 
 
• 
Provide public assistance with information regarding plats, aerial photos, construction drawings, 
utility locations, bench marks, property lines, and survey control. 
 
• 
Coordinate with the City’s GIS Department for performing GPS Surveys and data acquisition. 
 
• 
Determine angles and set grade stakes, hubs, turning points, and bench marks using appropriate 
surveying techniques; locate and measure boundary lines, rights-of-way, and measure land 
grades; maintain accurate survey records in the form of filed notes and sketches.  Perform land 
surveys for property line, easement, and right-of-way locations, water rights, aerial photography, 
monitoring wells, and bench mark establishment. 
 
• 
Provide research of City and County records for deeds, easements, and other documents to 
determine ownership and rights to property.

FIRST AMENDMENT TO CITY CONTRACT 7118 
 THE MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING BETWEEN 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND SADDLE RIDGE, LLC 
 
1. 
Parties.  This First Amendment to the Memorandum of Understanding (the "Amendment") 
is made and entered into by and between Saddle Ridge, LLC (“Saddle Ridge”), 506 Shoshoni 
Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, 82009, and the City of Cheyenne, a municipality organized and 
existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming (“City”), 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, 82001. 
 
2. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this Amendment is to extend the time for performance of the 
party’s respective obligations described in the Memorandum of Understanding due to mutual 
delays relating to Covid 19. 
 
3. 
Recitals. 
 
 
a. 
Saddle Ridge and the City entered into a Memorandum of Understanding, also 
known as City Contract number 7118, with an effective date of January 24, 2020 (the “MOU”). 
 
 
b. 
The MOU outlined each party’s responsibilities for certain improvements related to 
the construction of the Saddle Ridge Subdivision, 7th and 9th Filings.  The MOU states that it 
shall not extend past December 31, 2021. 
 
4. 
Amendment.  For good and valuable consideration, the receipt of which is hereby 
acknowledged, pursuant to paragraph 7(a) of the MOU, the parties hereby amend paragraph 3 of 
the MOU and extend the term of the MOU to December 31, 2022. 
 
5. 
No Other Amendments.  Except as set forth in this Amendment, the MOU shall remain 
unaffected and shall continue in full force and effect in accordance with its terms.  If there is 
conflict between this Amendment and the MOU, the terms of this MOU shall prevail. 
 
6. 
Signatures.  In witness whereof, the parties to this Amendment, through their duly 
authorized representatives, have executed this Amendment on the days and dates set out below 
and certify that they have read, understood and agreed to the terms and conditions of this 
Amendment as set forth herein. 
 
The effective date of this Amendment is the date of the signature last affixed to this page. 
 
(signature page follows) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 3:22 pm, Feb 08, 2022

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
January 18, 2022   
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-7-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and 
S&S Builders, LLC, for the Street and Alley Department Tent Building Repairs, 
and Door Repairs project 
______________________________________________ 
 
A Mandatory Pre-Bid Meeting was held for this project, and one (1) bidder was in attendance for 
the meeting:  S & S Builders, LLC. 
 
One (1) bidder responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  
 
The One (1) bid was received from: 
 
1. S & S Builders, LLC.  Cheyenne, Wyoming 
 
Bid S-7-22 also contained repairs to the tent building located at the City of Cheyenne Landfill.  
The week of the Bid Opening, winds caused further extensive damage far beyond the quantities 
and scope of work listed in the Bidding Documents.  At this time, the Public Works department is 
only considering the repairs at the Street and Alley Department, and is reviewing options for 
repairs at the landfill.  This partial award is identified on the Bid Tabulation.   
 
The bid was reviewed by Craig LaVoy (Public Works) & Matt Theriault (Public Works) and their 
recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from S & S Builders, LLC, for a total bid 
award amount of $105,104.69 for the Street and Alley Department Tent Building Repairs, and 
Door Repairs project.

mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Street and Alley Department Tent Building Repairs, and 
Door Repairs (Bid Line Items #1 & #12-24)” project all in strict accordance with the Contract 
Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), dated 
November 23, 2021. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by September 8, 2022.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of One 
Hundred Five Thousand, One Hundred Four Dollars, and Sixty-Nine Cents ($105,104.69).  
In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices 
multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted 
accordingly.  The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following 
manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated work completed.  
Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due date as established 
annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request shall be submitted on the Contract 
Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  
The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to payment. 
By Stefanie Boster at 10:25 pm, Jan 06, 2022

b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work completed for a 
minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement has been published 
in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the work under this Contract, 
the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; the Consent of Surety; 
Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and 
Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a current Workers 
Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be made until the above documents have 
been received by the City and all items on the Punch List have been completed, and the 
advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following:

2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders;

Provisions; and

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-14_TotalExtraction_67126109.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
January 18, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and KONE, Inc., for the 
repair of the South East Hydraulic Elevator located at the Jack Spiker Parking 
Garage 
______________________________________________ 
This is a single source purchase through KONE, Inc., therefore no bids were requested. 
The City of Cheyenne is currently under Contract with KONE, Inc. to provide elevator 
maintenance for the Jack Spiker Parking Garage, therefore the City is engaging KONE, Inc. to 
provide the necessary repairs as they will have continued responsibility for the maintenance of the 
elevator. 
The primary scope of work for the repairs is to furnish and install a new hydraulic power unit for 
the elevator.  
The City of Cheyenne, Facilities Maintenance Division, has provided the following notes to 
document the history of the elevator: 
1. The Spiker Parking Garage structure was built in 2004 and the equipment is original to the 
building. 
2. The elevator is under contract with KONE and receives quarterly inspections. 
3. All city elevators are under contract for maintenance with KONE except One (1) which is 
under contract with Schindler. 
4. The assumption is that the elevator has been abused by transients over the course of its life 
and has become problematic recently. 
The total request purchase amount is $42,665.00 
 
Funding: 
General Fund Reserves / $42,665.00 

February 7, 2022
January 24 & February 14, 2022
$42,665.00
By TJ Barttelbort at 3:10 pm, Jan 14, 2022

REQUEST TO PURCHASE OVER $35,000.00 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and KONE, Inc., for the repair of the 
South East Hydraulic Elevator located at the Jack Spiker Parking Garage, in an amount of 
$42,665.00.  This is a single source purchase, therefore no bids were requested.   
 
This will be reviewed by the City Council Finance Committee on February 7, 2022 and voted on 
at the February 14, 2022 City Council meeting. 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming 
(307) 773-1045 
 
Published in:  Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
Dates: January 20 & 27, 2022 

KONE Care™ 
 
PEOPLE FLOW REPAIR AND UPGRADE PROPOSAL  
 
 
 
 
REV 06/16 
Page 1 of 4 
 
January 10, 2022 
 
City of Cheyenne 
Jack Spiker Parking Garage 
322 W. Lincoln Way 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
 
Attn: Steve 
 
KONE Inc. 
8585 Concord Center Drive 
Suite 900 
Englewood, CO 80112 
www.kone.com 
scott.prussia@kone.com 
 
Description of Work: Power Unit and Oil 
We propose to furnish and install the labor, materials, tools and supervision to perform the following work on the 
South East Hydraulic Elevator located at the Jack Spiker Parking Garage in Cheyenne, WY. 
We will furnish and install a new hydraulic power unit.  The motor and pump will be submersed under the oil 
inside the tank in order to provide for sound isolation. A muffler, designed to reduce pulsation and noise, which 
may be present in the flow of hydraulic oil, will be provided in the oil line at the top of the pump. 
Control valves, including a safety check valve, an up direction valve with high pressure relief including up 
leveling and soft stop features and a lowering valve including down leveling and manual leveling feature, will be 
mounted in a compact unit assembly. A shut off valve, designed to shut off the flow of oil between the cylinder 
and the Power Unit, will be provided in the oil line in the machine room. Automatic two-way leveling will be 
provided to automatically stop and maintain the car approximately level with the landing, regardless of change 
in load. 
 

KONE Care™ 
 
PEOPLE FLOW REPAIR AND UPGRADE PROPOSAL  
 
 
 
 
REV 06/16 
Page 2 of 4 
 
 
 
New AW32 hydraulic oil will be furnished and installed.  The existing oil will be pumped out and removed of 
properly. 
 
 
 
Price 
Our total price to perform the above-mentioned work amounts to $42,665.00 exclusive of sales taxes.   
 
. 
Our price includes applicable labor, material and permit fees. This proposal is not binding on KONE until 
approved by an authorized KONE representative. Pricing is subject to KONE’s attached Terms and Conditions 
for tendered repairs and, by signing below, Purchaser hereby agrees to these Terms and Conditions. Price is 
valid for 30 days from the date of this proposal.   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

KONE Care™

TERMS AND CONDITIONS 
This proposal is subject to the following terms and conditions, all of which are hereby agreed to: 
KONE shall submit invoices for the value of material delivered and/or labor performed, less the down payment paid at the time of proposal acceptance. A final invoice shall 
be issued by KONE upon completion of the work and shall include all balances due. Purchaser agrees to pay the amount of any tax imposed by any existing law, or by any 
law enacted after the date of this Agreement, based upon the transfer, use, ownership or possession of the equipment involved in the services rendered herein. KONE 
reserves the right to discontinue our work at anytime until we have assurance, satisfactory to us, that payments will be made as agreed. Final payment shall become due 
and payable upon completion of the work described in this Agreement. Failure to pay any sum due to KONE within thirty (30) days of the invoice will be a material breach. A 
delinquent payment charge calculated at the rate of 1½ % per month, or if such rate is usurious then at the maximum rate under applicable law, shall be applied to the 
delinquent payments. In the event of default on the payment provisions herein, Purchaser agrees to pay, in addition to any defaulted amount, all attorney fees, collection cost 
or court costs in connection therewith. The machinery, implements and apparatus furnished hereunder remain KONE’s personal property and KONE retains title thereto until 
final payment is made, with right to retake possession of the same at the cost of the Purchaser if default is made in any of the payments, irrespective of the manner of 
attachment to the realty, the acceptance of notes, or the sale, mortgage or lease of the premises. 
The states requiring notice prior to filing a lien, this notice requirement is hereby complied with. 
KONE shall not be liable for damage or delay caused directly or indirectly by accidents, embargoes, strikes, lockouts, work interruption or other labor dispute, fire, theft, 
floods, or any cause beyond KONE’s control.  Regardless of the type of delay, KONE shall not be liable for any indirect, consequential, or special damages including but not 
limited to fines, penalties, loss of profits, goodwill, business or loss of use of equipment or property. 
Purchaser agrees to provide safe access to the equipment and machine room areas. Should conditions develop beyond KONE’s control, making the building or premises in 
which KONE’s personnel are working unsafe, KONE reserves the right to discontinue work until such unsafe conditions are corrected.  Should damage occur to KONE’s 
material or work on the premises, by fire, theft or otherwise, Purchaser shall compensate us therefore. 
Notwithstanding anything contained to the contrary within this proposal, KONE’s work shall not include any abatement or disturbance of asbestos containing material (ACM), 
presumed asbestos containing materials (PACM) or other hazardous materials (i.e. lead, PCBs) (collectively “HazMat”). KONE shall have the right to discontinue its work in 
any location where suspected HazMat is encountered or disturbed.  Any HazMat removal or abatement, or delays caused by such, required in order for KONE to perform its 
work shall be the Customer’s sole responsibility and expense.  
 KONE undertakes to perform this work in conformity with the usual applied codes and standards, however, no guarantee can be made that all code violations or defects 
have been found.  This work is not intended as a guarantee against failure or malfunction of equipment at any future time. 
It is agreed and understood that KONE is not responsible for damages, either to the vertical transportation equipment or to the building, or for any personal injury or death, 
arising from or resulting from any code required safety tests performed on this equipment. 
Nothing in this agreement shall be construed to mean that KONE assumes any liability of any nature whatsoever arising out of, relating to or in any way connected with the 
use or operation of the equipment described above.  Purchaser shall be solely responsible for the use, repair and maintenance of the equipment and for taking such steps 
including but not limited to providing attendant personnel, warning signs and other controls necessary to ensure the safety of the user or safe operation of the equipment. 
Neither KONE nor its affiliates, subsidiaries or divisions shall be responsible or liable for any damages, claims, suits, expenses and payments on account of or resulting from 
any injury, death or damage to property arising or resulting from the misuse, abuse or neglect of the equipment herein named or any other device covered by this contract. 
Purchaser shall at all times and at Purchaser’s own cost, maintain a commercial general liability policy covering bodily injury and property damage with the limits of liability 
Purchasers customarily carry (naming KONE as additional insured) arising out of the services provided under this Authorization and/or the ownership, maintenance, use or 
operation of the equipment described herein.  
It is agreed and understood that Purchaser is solely responsible for ongoing maintenance and care of the equipment described above.  IT IS EXPRESSLY UNDERSTOOD, 
IN CONSIDERATION OF OUR PERFORMANCE OF THIS WORK THAT PURCHASER ASSUMES ALL LIABILITY FOR THE USE, MAINTENANCE OR OPERATION OF 
THE EQUIPMENT DESCRIBED ABOVE AND FOR ANY INJURY, INCLUDING DEATH, TO ANY PERSON OR PERSONS AND FOR DAMAGE TO PROPERTY OR 
LOSS OF USE THEREOF, ON ACCOUNT OF OR RESULTING FROM THE PERFORMANCE OF THE WORK TO BE DONE HEREIN, AND AGREES TO THE EXTENT 
PERMITTED BY LAW TO DEFEND, INDEMNIFY AND HOLD HARMLESS KONE, ITS OFFICERS, DIRECTORS AND EMPLOYEES FROM ALL DAMAGES, CLAIMS, 
SUITS, EXPENSES AND PAYMENTS ON ACCOUNT OF OR RESULTING FROM ANY SUCH INJURY, DEATH OR DAMAGE TO PROPERTY, EXCEPT THAT 
RESULTING FROM THE SOLE NEGLIGENCE OF KONE INC.  Purchaser hereby waives any and all rights of recovery, arising as a matter of law or otherwise, which 
Purchaser might now or hereafter have against KONE Inc. 
KONE warrants the materials and workmanship of the equipment for 90 days after completion. Purchaser’s remedy is limited to repair or replacement of a defective part, in 
KONE’s sole discretion. The warranty is limited to the replacement or repair of the part itself, and excludes labor. In no event shall KONE be responsible for damage due to 
normal wear and tear, vandalism, abuse, misuse, neglect, work or repairs or modifications by others, or any other cause beyond the control of KONE. KONE disclaims any 
other warranty of any kind, either expressed or implied, including without limitation the implied warranties of merchantability or fitness for a particular purpose, or 
noninfringement. 
Unless otherwise agreed, it is understood that the work shall be performed during regular working hours of regular working days of the elevator trade. If overtime work is 
mutually agreed upon and performed, the additional price, at KONE’s usual rates for such work, shall be added to the contract price herein named. 
It is expressly understood and agreed all prior agreements written or verbal regarding the subject matter herein are void and the acceptance of this Agreement shall 
constitute the contract for the material and work specified in this Agreement. Any changes to this Agreement must be made in writing and signed by both parties. 
The terms and conditions set forth herein shall constitute the complete agreement for any work performed, AND shall prevail over and supersede any terms and conditions 
contained in any documents provided by the Purchaser. 
The Purchaser does hereby agree the exclusive venue for any dispute between the parties shall be in the county of Rock Island, IL.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-02-14_TotalExtraction_17214078.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 14, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave., and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Ken Esquibel 
(via Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal and Jeff White.  
Absent: Mr. Cook and Mr. Segrave.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 
PROCLAMATION – Designating February 12-19, 2022 as Entrepreneurship Week.  
Mayor Collins read the proclamation into the record and expressed appreciation to members of the 
Innovation and Entrepreneur Advisory Council who were in attendance. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #25(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. Roybal, 
agenda item #17 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Johnson moved to approve 
remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 24, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for 521 Southwest Drive (located east of 
Southwest Drive, south of I-80).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, provided 
information on the property and reviewed requirements of compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes.  Mr. Lloyd noted the affiliated annexation and zone change ordinances 
are also on the agenda.  Upon request, Mr. Lloyd provided examples of developments for the 
Mixed-Use Employment zone category.  There being no further comments or input and purpose 
of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the Zoning Classification from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis for land located northwest of the intersection of Nationway and Logan Avenue.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  (#4415) 

 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 13.04 – Public Utilities Generally; 
Chapter 13.08 – Water Service System; Chapter 13.12 – Sewer System; Chapter 13.16 – Storm 
Sewers; and Chapter 13.20 – Wastewater Treatment & Disposal; of Title 13 – Public Services, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved 
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts 
and Uses, amending the Permissibility of Various Uses – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80 – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor 
– Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural for 
land located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80 – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to HI Heavy 
Industrial for land located north of Campstool Road, west of Progress Circle – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Creating a new Section 9.08.120, Malicious Harassment 
Based on Race, Color, Religion, Ancestry, Sex, Sexual Orientation, Gender Identity and 
Expression, National Origin, or Disability, of Chapter 9.08, Offenses Against Public Peace and 
Decency, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – 
referred to Committee of the Whole. (Sponsors – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, 
Mr. Roybal and Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.20, Cemeteries, 
of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne pertaining 
to Cemetery fees – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Segrave and Mr. White) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing up to a $14,890,000 interfund loan from the One Percent 
Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to the Special Purpose Option Sales Tax Capital Projects Fund to 
facilitate the construction of three fire stations, purchase fire trucks and replace the cemetery 
irrigation system as approved by voters on the November 2, 2021 Sixth Penny Ballot.  Jim 

Ridgeway advised he is in support of the project and inquired as to when construction would take 
place.  John Kopper, Chief of the Fire Department, advised the city has a Professional Services 
Agreement with Plan One Architects and the projects are almost shovel ready, estimating the first 
fire station to be ready for construction by June.  Upon inquiry, Chief Kopper provided the 
following information: the public areas of the fire stations will meet ADA requirements, each staff 
member on duty will have their own bedroom in the new fire stations and there will be 3 separate 
bathrooms.  County Commissioner Gunner Malm expressed appreciation to Mayor Collins and 
the governing body for looking at this funding option, noting it is a great opportunity to save 
taxpayers money on the projects and to everyone who provided information to the public on the 
6th Penny projects as they are instrumental in the community.  Mayor Collins advised that due to 
inflation, the cost will continue to rise for construction of the fire stations and this approach 
provides for financial responsibility and accountability to the voters.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Concerns and comments were made relating to the Consent Agenda 
process regarding public comment options.  City Treasurer Robin Lockman, upon inquiry, advised 
this type of funding option was utilized in the past for construction of the David R. Romero Park.  
Ms. Lockman further advised the funds come out of 5th Penny fund, provided information on 
available funding in the 5th Penny fund and noted it will not affect reserves.  Mayor Collins 
explained utilizing this funding option includes repayment to the fund with interest.  Appreciation 
was expressed to Ms. Lockman and her team for their outstanding services and their assistance 
with the resolution.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
(#6207) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of a Federal Emergency Management Agency 
Preparedness Grant under the Staffing for Adequate Fire and Emergency Response (SAFER) 
Program, on behalf of the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, for the purpose of adding nine 
new firefighter positions.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6208) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Certifying Planning Commission Action amending the Future Land Use 
Maps of PlanCheyenne changing the land use category from Community Business to Mixed-Use 
Commercial for property generally located west of Campbell Avenue, north of Nationway, and 
south of and adjacent to E. Lincolnway.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6209) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting the revised City of Cheyenne, Wyoming Investment Policy 
Statement – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor- Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and file the Wyoming 
Division of Victim Services Funding Application State Fiscal Year 2023 and 2024, July 1, 2022 
to June 30, 2024 – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Sweetgrass, 9th Filing, a Replat of all of Lot 1, Block 4, Sweetgrass, 1st Filing situated in the N1/2 
of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located south of East Murray Drive west and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive) – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook)  
 

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Adopting a fee schedule for Planning and Development projects – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – For Governing Body approval of a one-year Bronze Boot Sponsor 
relationship between Cheyenne Frontier Days and Miller/Coors for 2022 – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White)  
 
 
Grant of Right-of-Way Easement on City Owned Property, per Exhibit A, between the City 
of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power Company for a 150’ wide right-of-way and 
easement for construction and maintenance of overhead power facilities.  Jim Wasson, Cheyenne 
Light Fuel and Power Company, advised he was available for any questions.  Charles Bloom, 
Director of Planning and Development, requested the item be postponed to allow for research on 
the $10 amount affiliated with the easement.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Mr. White moved to postpone to February 28, 2022, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. 
Wasson explained the $10 fee is just a placeholder as compensation must be put towards the 
agreement. He further noted landowners in the vicinity pay $15/linear foot.  Motion to postpone 
carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) between City of Cheyenne and Doran Lummis to 
authorize filing of platting applications(s) for City-owned lands for the purpose of access to future 
development.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7472) 
 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Thomas Szott for City 
Prosecutor legal counsel services (General Fund).  Mr. White advised the item was reviewed under 
other items for the agenda at the February 7, 2022 Finance Committee meeting, therefore, 
provisions of Title 2 of City Code have been met.  Mr. White moved to adopt in an amount not to 
exceed $86,400.00 annually, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised Mr. 
Szott will serve as the City Prosecutor and expressed the need to fill the position is immediate.   
City Attorney Stefanie Boster explained, upon inquiry, that the intent is for Mr. Szott to serve in a 
part time capacity to allow him time to work on his private practice, noting currently due to the 
demand of city cases, he is currently working full time.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#7473) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Kyle M. Brandon for 
City Public Defender legal counsel services (General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Veolia ES Technical 
Solutions, LLC, for the Hazardous Waste Disposal Services Project (Solid Waste Fund) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Jones Land 
Surveying, Inc., for professional Land Surveying Services (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred 
to Finance Committee. 
 

[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Steil Surveying 
Services, LLC, for professional Land Surveying Services (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) – referred to 
Finance Committee.   
 
 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #6957 between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming 
Cattle Co., LLC adding the Mariah Pasture and extending the term of the Belvoir Ranch Grazing 
Lease Agreement through December 31, 2024 (Revenue to the City).  Mr. White advised the 
recommendation of the Finance Committee was to approve in an amount not to exceed 
$187,477.50, however, it has been brought to his attention that due to the nature of the contract, 
the amount of revenue may fluctuate annually depending on the condition of the grass and the 
amount of grazing.  Mr. White moved to approve in the amount of $187,477.50, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Upon inquiry, Craig LaVoy, Deputy Director of Public Works, explained the Mariah 
Pasture is no longer considered over-grazed and the Conservation District supports continuation 
of grazing.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6957) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6441 between the City of Cheyenne and Microsoft 
Corporation for the renewal to the City’s Enterprise Office 365 Online Enrollment.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6441) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7118 for the Memorandum of Understanding 
between the City of Cheyenne and Saddle Ridge, LLC, to outline the responsibilities for the 
donation of land and for the management of a multi-purpose non-motorized pedestrian and bicycle 
pathway, park and landscape amenities in a portion of Saddle Ridge, 7th and 9th Filings 
(Community Facility Fees) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7119 for the Memorandum of Understanding 
between the City of Cheyenne and WJE, LLC, to outline the responsibilities for the donation of 
land and for the management of a multi-purpose non-motorized pedestrian and bicycle pathway, 
park and landscape amenities in a portion of Saddle Ridge, 8th, 9th, 10th, 11th, 12th and 13th Filings 
(Community Facility Fees) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-7-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and S&S 
Builders, LLC, for the Street and Alley Department Tent Building Repairs and Door Repairs 
Project (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid in an 
amount not to exceed $105,104.69). (#7474)  
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-8-22 for a Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Front 
Range Roofing Systems, LLC, for the Ice and Events Center Roof Replacement Project (General 
Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid in an amount not to exceed 
$181,650.00). (#7475) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and KONE, Inc., for the repair 
of the South East Hydraulic Elevator located at the Jack Spiker Parking Garage (General Fund 
Reserves).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $42,655.00). 
 

[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-11-22 for furnishing eight (8) light poles (17th Street Lighting), 
for the City of Cheyenne, Engineering Department (Specific Purpose Sales Tax) – referred to 
Finance Committee. 
               
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
Announcement was made that due to the Presidents Day holiday on Monday, February 21, 
2022, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held as follows: 
Finance Committee will meet at Noon on Tuesday, February 22, 2022 and Public Services 
Committee will meet at Noon on Wednesday, February 23, 2022 at the Kiwanis Community House 
at 4603 Lions Park Drive, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting. 
 
Announcement was made of a Committee of the Whole meeting to be held Wednesday, 
February 23, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, 
Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting. 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, 
limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, special malt beverage 
and winery permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.    
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for Dillon Companies, LLC, a Kansas limited liability company d/b/a King 
Soopers, 3702 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Avanti, Inc., 
d/b/a Dell Range Liquor, 3806 Dell Range Blvd. #4, Cheyenne, Wyoming)  
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, to hear public comment regarding the City’s intent to enter into a contract 
with Comea Shelter to grant a subaward from Coronavirus State and Local Fiscal Recovery Funds 
to be used to increase available emergency housing and temporary shelter for Cheyenne’s 
homeless population. 
 
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Upon inquiry by Mayor Collins, City Clerk Kris Jones advised the following item was 
being introduced for the purpose of being referred to Finance Committee to be held on February 
22, 2022 at the Kiwanis Community House:  Contract between the City of Cheyenne and the 
Comea Shelter granting a subaward to be used to increase available emergency housing and 
temporary shelter to support Cheyenne’s homeless population.  (Coronavirus State and Local 
Fiscal Recovery Funds) Mayor Collins referred the item to the Finance Committee.   
    
    
 

 
Jim Ridgeway expressed concern relating to residential sewer fees being billed for 35 day 
periods and how the extra days affect the budgeted bill amount.  Mr. Ridgeway advised the Board 
of Public Utilities explained the late readings were due to staffing issues within Zone 17.  Mr. 
Ridgeway noted he does not believe the consumer should have to pay for the inefficiencies.  Mayor 
Collins advised water and sewer rates are established during the winter months.  Mr. Ridgeway 
provided photos and information regarding Lions Park walking paths and advised there are issues 
with standing water and drainage problems which freezes and causes safety hazards.  Mr. 
Ridgeway inquired about the Bird Scooters and requirements of the company relating to picking 
up abandon scooters.  Mayor Collins advised users can leave them in areas around town and the 
company is responsible for retrieving and maintaining them.  Mr. Johnson provided detailed 
information regarding reporting concerns involving the scooters and advised residents can find 
additional information on the city’s and Bird Rides’ websites.  Mr. Johnson provided additional 
information relating to violations of city code involving the scooters.  Mr. Ridgeway advised he 
believes it is a burden on citizens to contact the company.  Compliance Director Eric Fountain, 
upon inquiry, reported an annual review will be held soon to address any issues involving the 
scooters.  Deputy Community Recreation and Events Director Jason Sanchez, upon inquiry, 
provided information relating to snow removal in Lions Parks and advised he would look into 
drainage concerns.  Mr. Ridgeway further referenced a memo provided by City Treasurer Robin 
Lockman on January 18th related to the resolution authorizing the interfund loan for various 
November 2, 2021 Sixth Penny Ballot projects and the funding of the 2019-2022 5th Penny ballot, 
noting overages collected that could be allotted for other needed improvements. 
 
 
Dr. Aldrich advised there will be a City Council Ward III Council and Coffee event on 
Saturday, February 19th, from 9 am - 11 am at Dazbog Coffee (3911 E. Pershing Blvd.).  
 
 
Mr. Johnson commended colleagues for their attendance and participation at the annual 
Boys & Girls Club Chili Cook and expressed appreciation to Triumph High Culinary Club for 
their 3rd Place win.  Mayor Collins advised the fundraising event included a friendly competition 
between members of the Governing Body and the County Commissioners.  Dr.  Aldrich reported 
the event raised almost $20k between the fundraising efforts of the Governing Body and the 
County Commissioners. 
 
Mr. Roybal moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4-
405(a)(x), seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.  Mayor Collins advised the executive session will be held to discuss the upcoming 
annual Fire Union contract and announced members would meet in executive session, not open to 
the public; requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, City Treasurer, Chief of Staff and Fire Chief 
be in attendance and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the 
executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close 
of the executive session. (Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 7:02 p.m.).     
 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 7:58 p.m. 
 
 

 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:58 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
February 25, 2022 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2021-08-09_TotalExtraction_10562261.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 26, 2021 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 12:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Ken Esquibel (via Zoom), 
Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson (via Zoom), Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne (joined via Zoom at 
12:06 p.m.), Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, Deputy City 
Clerk.  
 
Following the pledge of allegiance, Mayor Collins called for a moment of silence for 
Senator Mike Enzi related to injuries recently sustained in a serious bicycle accident.  
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 12, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive 
(Postponed from July 12, 2021). Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Laybourn moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Mr. Laybourn advised that 
following the Laramie County Board of Commissioner’s meeting on July 20, 2021, the plat was 
reconsidered but not approved.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Mayor Collins explained the indefinite postponement essentially kills the item, therefore 
requiring it to come back before the governing body in the future.  Motion to postpone indefinitely 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential to MR 
Medium-Density Residential for land located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, 
Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive (Postponed from July 12, 2021).  Mr. Laybourn moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Laybourn advised that following 
the Laramie County Board of Commissioner’s meeting on July 20, 2021, the plat was reconsidered 
but not approved.  Mr. Laybourn moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
to postpone indefinitely carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 

   
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road. 
(Postponed from July 12, 2021).  Mr. Laybourn moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Laybourn moved to amend by substitute dated July 16, 2021, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mayor Collins noted the substitute includes the Dell Range Blvd.  
intersection on the map.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”. [Clerks note: Dr. Rinne joined the 
meeting at 12:06 p.m. during discussion and prior to the vote on this item] (#4387) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use and County LR Low 
Density Residential to CB Community Business, LR Low-Density Residential, and MR Medium-
Density Residential for land located northeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range 
Boulevard, west of Whitney Road (Postponed from July 12, 2021).  Mr. Laybourn moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body. (#4388) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, Laramie County, 
Wyoming generally located southwest of Interstate 25 and Happy Jack Road.  Mr. Laybourn 
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4389) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County X (Exempt) to SS (Support Services) 
for a portion of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, 
Laramie County, Wyoming generally located southwest of Interstate 25 and Happy Jack Road. 
Mr. Laybourn moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4390)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Medium-Density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential-Medium Density for land located northwest of the intersection of 
Country Club Avenue and Snyder Avenue.  Mr. Laybourn moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, Charles Bloom, Planning and Development 
Director, reported the zone change request is affiliated with several different applications and 
multiple variances were being considered by the Board of Adjustment including a reduction of 
parking, necessary setbacks and access of which several elements were denied.  Mr. Bloom advised 
work with the developer is ongoing to redo the site plan and plat.  He noted they are still 
recommending approval of the zone change to NR-2 as they believe it is the correct zoning for the 
neighborhood and will allow for existing development to continue and for future redevelopment.  
Upon further inquiry, Mr. Bloom explained the developer will be retaining the existing building 
and improving the gravel parking lot and noted they are proposing 4 new townhouse units along 
Snyder Ave.  Mr. Bloom advised the Board of Adjustment denied the request for 3 of the 

   
townhouse units to have rear access and 1 to have a driveway access on Snyder. During discussion 
related to comments and concerns of area neighbors, Mayor Collins noted he had received a 
records request for information related to the project and comments were received related to 
drainage concerns and support for the added units but concerns with the space restraint.  Mayor 
Collins advised he met with the developer’s representative and they are aware of drainage issues 
and are working on an engineering solution.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom advised zoning in the area 
is MR (Medium Density Residential) and is complementary to the requested NR-2 zoning 
classification.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4391) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Public Safety Fee requirement for residential, commercial, service, industrial, civic, public 
service, employment, agriculture, and other development in the city.  Charles Bloom, Planning 
and Development Director, advised he met with members of the Southeast Wyoming Home 
Builders Association and although they aren’t totally in support of the ordinance, they do agree 
this is an important first step and the ordinance will need to be revisited in the future.  Future 
review will involve the association and review of the fees to make sure they are accurate.  Mr. 
Bloom noted the fees will be for public safety infrastructure and not an offset to the Fire or Police 
Department’s general operating costs.  Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to the Southeast 
Wyoming Home Builders Association for their contributions related to the ordinance.  Mr. 
Laybourn moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Comment was 
made related to the need to be able to maintain and provide public safety services in all areas of 
the community and appreciation was expressed to Mr. Bloom and the Southeast Wyoming Home 
Builders Association for working on the ordinance.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. (#4392)  
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members-Term-Vacancies, 
of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Laybourn moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Comments were made regarding improvements that have been 
made downtown and the importance of the ordinance for streamlining downtown efforts.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception Mr. Johnson voting 
“no”.  Mayor Collins noted that due to the ordinance change, applications for the board will be 
accepted for appointment. (#4393) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events 
Department to submit a proposal to apply for an Office of Local Defense Community Cooperation, 
Department of Defense, Defense Community Infrastructure Program Grant to build an indoor 
sports complex to support the military family quality of life Infrastructure of Francis E. Warren 
Air Force Base Service Members and their families.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#6163) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Waterford 
Square 3rd Filing, a replat of Lots 28 & 29, Block 7, Lots 1 & 31, Block 5 and a portion of Tray 
More Boulevard and adjacent alleys, Waterford Square, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located south of the intersection of Tray More Boulevard and Waterford Boulevard). 
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6164) 

   
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to sign a final plat for Sweetgrass, 
7th Filing, a subdivision of a portion of the N½ of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West 
of the 6th P. M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie county, Wyoming (located south of East College Drive 
and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive). Mr. Laybourn moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Mr. 
Laybourn moved to postpone until August 9, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Upon inquiry, 
Charles Bloom, Planning and Development Director, explained the reason for the postponement 
is to review a new plat that was submitted by the agent for the owner.  Motion to postpone carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing a one-time payment given to the full-time and permanent 
part-time employees of the City of Cheyenne (excluding elected officials and employees of the 
Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities) from funds received from the Coronavirus State and Local 
Fiscal Recovery Fund Established by the American Rescue Plan Act of 2021 – referred to Finance 
Committee.  (Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Segrave, and Mr. 
White.)  
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County, Wyoming, and the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, for EOD, SWAT, and CNU operations and extraterritorial law enforcement 
assistance and activities.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7376) 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and SpyGlass Group, LLC, Westlake, OH, for 
communications services auditing.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7377) 
 
[CA] 
Merchant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and RecTrac, LLC d/b/a Vermont 
Systems, for credit card processing of Recreation related services.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#7378) 
 
[CA] 
Grant of Access Easement and Temporary Construction Easement on City owned Property, 
Tract U, Sweetgrass, 2nd Filing, between the City of Cheyenne and Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC - 
referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives, and Laramie County, 
Wyoming for juvenile probation services (revenue to the City) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7231 between the City of Cheyenne and Burns and 
McDonnell for a force majeure for additional architectural and inspection services for the Landfill 
Front Entrance and Scale House Construction Support Project (Solid Waste Fund).  Reporting for 
Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal advised there was no recommendation from committee.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $38,937.00, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Comments were made relating to the possibility of rebidding the project.  Upon inquiry, Craig 
LaVoy, Deputy Director of Public Works, explained the contract is for the project design and the 
consequence of requesting a new bid proposal is the project would be shut down.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. 
Esquibel voting “no”. (#7231) 
 

   
[CA] 
Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6625 between the City of Cheyenne and Western 
Research and Development, Ltd., for additional design services for the 2018 On Call Traffic 
Engineering Services Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(in an amount not to exceed $35,194.51). (#6625) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-40-21 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Goat 
Green, LLC, for providing vegetation management for Dry Creek and Crow Creek, for the City of 
Cheyenne Engineering Department (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 
Appointment of Julie Torres to the Laramie County Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board.   
Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral 
appointment).  
 
 
Announcement was made of Public Hearing to be held August 9, 2021 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference 
meeting, to receive comments and input for the Hitching Post Urban Renewal Plan and Project, as 
required by Wyoming Statute §15-9-109, for land generally located northwest of West Lincolnway 
and Stinson Avenue.       
 
 
Under other business: 
 
 
Mayor Collins noted that at the July 12, 2021 City Council meeting, the motion to approve 
the Memorandum of Agreement between Robin and Debbie Courtier, the City of Cheyenne, and 
the Wyoming Department of Transportation for the study of downstream water problems was 
approved. He further advised WYDOT was included in the MOU as they hold the easement for 
the pond that is being reviewed, but that the Attorney General is uncomfortable signing the 
agreement.  Mayor Collins advised he would like a motion to reconsider the agreement to remove 
WYDOT and they will do a direct agreement with the city on the modification of their easement.  
Mr. Segrave, having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant to City Code Chapter 2.04, moved 
to reconsider the Memorandum of Agreement between Robin and Debbie Courtier, the City of 
Cheyenne, and the Wyoming Department of Transportation for the study of downstream water 
problems, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Mr. Segrave moved to amend 
by substitute dated July 23, 2021, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mayor Collins advised the only change 
in the substitute is the removal of WYDOT as a signatory of the agreement making the agreement 
between the city and the Courtiers only.  Upon inquiry, Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, advised 
the Attorney General’s office has communicated concerns related to the agreement during a 
meeting and via email noting they aren’t comfortable signing the agreement in its original format.  
Upon request for more information on the project, Mayor Collins noted he will look into 
scheduling an informational work session.  Concern was raised about WYDOT being removed 

   
from the agreement.  Mayor Collins described issues with the original agreement and noted he has 
received assurance that all parties want to be involved with the project.  Motion to amend by 
substitute caried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. 
Rinne voting “no”.  Appreciation was expressed to the governing body for their consideration to 
remedy the situation.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#7374) 
 
 
Dr. Aldrich expressed appreciation to Mr. Laybourn and the DDA for their beautification 
efforts in the downtown area.  She further thanked Jason Sanchez, Deputy Director of Community 
Recreation and Events, and his staff for their assistance with Fridays on the Plaza and city staff for 
their efforts downtown. 
 
 
Mr. Laybourn noted the extra effort downtown was not unnoticed.  He further expressed 
appreciation to Public Works for the median clean-up by Little America.  Mayor Collins advised 
the median clean-up was a joint effort between Public Works and Community Recreation & 
Events.  Mayor Collins further expressed appreciation to city employees for their efforts in keeping 
the city safe and appealing. 
 
 
Mr. Johnson noted the recent passing of Bill Dubois, local historian, and expressed 
appreciation for all he did for the community. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 12:44 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, CMC  
Deputy City Clerk

Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
