ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM MR 
MEDIUM-DENSITY 
RESIDENTIAL 
TO 
NR-2 
NEIGHBORHOOD 
RESIDENTIAL – MEDIUM DENSITY FOR LAND LOCATED NORTH OF AND 
ADJACENT TO MILLER LANE, BETWEEN SUNSET DRIVE AND TOWNSEND
PLACE.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
Section 2. That on August 2, 2021, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change. 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Lots 7-18, Block 3,
Miller Addition is hereby changed to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential - Medium Density. 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
AUGUST 2, 2021 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE:
UDC-21-00256: Miller Addition, 5th Filing Zone Change 
 
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 1.6 acres of land from MR Medium-
density Residential to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential  Medium Density   
LOCATION: 
North of Miller Lane, west of Townsend Place 
APPLICANT: 
AVI- Brad Emmons 
AGENT: 
AVI- Brad Emmons 
OWNER: 
Naler Residential Properties LLC 
ZONING: 
MR Medium-density Residential 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential 
PURPOSE: 
To allow cottage lot development. 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00256
August 2, 2021
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lots 7-18, Block 3, Miller Addition to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential  
Medium Density as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review 
criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MR Medium-density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential  Medium Density for 1.6 acres of land. The land was once developed with a 
mobile home park, currently contains one single-family home, and is anticipated to be developed in the 
future with a set of duplexes utilizing a cottage lot configuration.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development and a mix of housing 
types in neighborhoods (although it is not the intent of the plan to change existing, stable 
neighborhoods).   
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Urban Residential  
MR (Medium-density 
Residential) 
Mostly vacant, one 
single-family home 
North 
Urban Residential   
MUB (Mixed-Use Business 
Emphasis)
Vacant and Preschool 
South 
Urban Residential   
MR 
Apartments, 
Single-family residential 
East 
Urban Residential   
MR 
Single-family residential, 
duplexes 
West 
Urban Residential   
MR 
Single-family residential, 
duplex 

UDC-21-00256
August 2, 2021
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential. According to 
comprehensive plan the optimal City zones for the future land use designation of Urban 
Residential are LR, MR, HR, NR-1, NR-2, and NR-3.  
 
Based on the policies and future land use designation of the subject property, NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential  Medium Density zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
This property, and most of the surrounding property, is currently zoned MR with the future land 
use designation of Urban Residential. The MR zone allows a variety of residential development, 
including apartments with conditional use approval, but the NR-2 zone allows smaller lot sizes 
and smaller multifamily without conditional use approval. The NR-2 zone also allows cottage lot 
style development. The Urban Residential future land use designation encourages a variety of 
housing types and densities. Existing development in the area includes apartment buildings and 
duplexes. The NR-2 zone is compatible with the surrounding area considering existing zoning 
and development patterns. 
 
The property adjacent to the north is zoned MUB and only partially developed. The MUB zone 
allows a variety of residential and commercial uses and is considered a higher-intensity zone 
than either the current MR zone or the proposed NR-2 zone. The rezoning of this property to 
NR-2 would not have an undue influence on the property to the north. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current MR zone allows apartments as a conditional use, larger townhomes, duplexes, and 
all but the smallest single-family home lots. The NR-2 zone allows larger apartments as a 
conditional use, smaller apartments, townhomes, duplexes, and the smallest style of single-
family lots, including Cottage Lots.  
 
While the surrounding area is all zoned MR, there are lots in the neighborhood which are too 
small to be established under the current regulations. The NR-2 zone would allow smaller lots 
which are compatible with existing development patterns. 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
 

UDC-21-00256
August 2, 2021
4)
The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The existing block is larger than anticipated for the urban-style of development anticipated for 
MUB or NR-2 zoning. However, the subject property is in an area previously developed and 
limited options are available to create meaningful connections to the street network. No 
additions to the street network are proposed.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The NR-2 zone is intended for a medium residential density and mix of dwelling types in a 
compact development pattern (with smaller, walkable blocks). 
 
While any residential development within the City is expected to meet the general residential 
design standards, the NR-2 zone has additional compact and mixed-density neighborhood 
design standards found in UDC 6.6.4. The MR zone allows wide driveways, provided they are 
setback from the side lot line, and allows the front façade to be dominated by the garage. The 
NR-2 zone requires more discrete access (narrower driveways or alley access) but allows 
driveways to be built to the side property line. Garages are prohibited from dominating the 
front façade. Existing development in the area is more reflective of the design standards for the 
MR zone than the design of the proposed NR-2 zone. A change to NR-2 may necessitate 
replatting of the subdivision for the best development capabilities.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The existing lots do not meet frontage requirements to have front access, a new alley or access 
easement would be required to be dedicated. Lots would become less non-conforming from a 
size and frontage aspect, but more nonconforming from an access standpoint. A rezone almost 
requires a replat. A replat has been proposed. 
 
While the NR-2 zone would give more flexibility in potential development (or redevelopment) of 
this parcel should the specific development proposed not be executed; rezoning to NR-2 creates 
difficulty in developing the existing lots, hence a replat likely being necessary to develop to the 
maximum extent practical.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
 

UDC-21-00256
August 2, 2021
7)
The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7)
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The NR-2 zone 
is appropriate based on the future land use designation for the subject property and surrounding 
development patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MR to NR-2.

Subject: UDC-21-00094 / Sweetgrass, 7th Filing / Final Plat

July 7, 2021 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Final Plat. This is approved by Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
x

x

Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their July 6, 2021 meeting and by a vote of 3-0, 
recommended the Governing Body approve this application.   
  
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed.  
 
The condition that was placed on the approval was modified at the Planning Commission meeting by 
Staff to read as follows:  
1. Prior to recording of the final plat, the applicant shall enter into a development agreement for 
public safety fees. Public safety fees shall be based on a January 2021 fee of $756 per unit and 
shall be adjusted for inflation utilizing the construction cost index for twenty (20) cities, as 
issued by the current Engineering News Record to the date of final plat filing. This condition shall 
not apply if City-wide public safety impact fees are adopted and applicable to this development 
prior to recording of this plat.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
July 6, 2021 
STAFF REPORT 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00094: Sweetgrass, 7th Filing Preliminary Plat (Expedited 
Request) 
REQUEST: 
A preliminary plat proposing creation of 161 lots. 
LOCATION: 
South of E. College Drive and East of Sweetgrass Drive.  
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma, Steil Surveying Services 
OWNER: 
Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC 
ZONING: 
Sweetgrass PUD (Ordinances 4232, 4286) 
PURPOSE: 
A preliminary plat proposing 149 residential lots, two Civic Lots, a Lot 
designated for future Multi-family Residential, and the remainder being 
designated as open space on a 94.06-acre parcel of land for future 
development 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00094  
 
 
July 6, 2021 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend exceptions for block sizes and approve the Sweetgrass 7th Filing Preliminary Plat 
subject to staff’s recommended conditions, noting that as conditioned, the project meets the review 
criteria for approval, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing Body. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval  
x 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
x 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for a 94.06-acre tract of land to create 161 lots for 
future residential development. 149 of the lots will be for residential development, one lot will be dedicated 
for a new school, one lot will be dedicated for a future joint Police and Fire Station and one lot will be for 
future Multi-family residential, while the remainder of the lots are dedicated as open space tracks for the 
integrated trail system. The land is currently vacant.  
 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is zoned as part of the Sweetgrass PUD.  The Sweetgrass PUD is generally described as “a 
±2,349.5-acre community envisioned as a distinctive mixed-use master planned village, integrating 
retail, entertainment, lodging, employment opportunities, and variety of intimately scaled residential 
neighborhoods.”  The district generally allows a broad range of uses. The MDR is intended to provide for 
residential development allowing for a variety of single-family detached and attached dwelling units, 
located nearby to schools and commercial centers. An extensive open space network should be 
provided with within each MDR parcel. To promote greater diversity, lower density development, such 
as that permitted within LDR districts, is permitted within MDR districts.

UDC-21-00094  
 
 
July 6, 2021 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Expedited Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d and UDC 2.1.3.d a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with a 
residential use. This plat proposes 149 residential lots with an incorporated trail system 
connecting this development to existing and future developments.  
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 2 of PlanCheyenne encourages new residential development to be compatible and 
connected with existing residential neighborhoods. The proposed plat ties into the adjacent 
platted neighborhoods.   
 
Foundation 4 of PlanCheyenne encourages a connected, multi-model transportation system that 
can accommodate future growth. The proposed plat connects to existing developments but is 
constrained by topography to the north. A proposed trail system increases the potential for 
pedestrian and cyclist connectivity. 
 
Foundation 6 of PlanCheyenne encourages the creation of open space and trails and the 
preservation of significant natural resources. The plat proposes dedicated open space lots that 
will be a part of an integrated trail system throughout Sweetgrass. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
This plat is one of the MDR zones of the Sweetgrass PUD. The MDR is intended to provide for 
residential development allowing for a variety of single-family detached and attached dwelling 
units, located nearby to schools and commercial centers. An extensive open space network 
should be provided within each MDR parcel. To promote greater diversity, lower density 
development, such as that permitted within LDR districts, is permitted within MDR districts. 
 
This plat also proposes a Lot designated for a Future Elementary School and a Lot designated for 
a Future Joint Police and Fire Station. The continuation of lots designated as open space for an 
integrated trail system are also provided.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   

UDC-21-00094  
 
 
July 6, 2021 
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network: 
The plat proposes dividing land Southeast of an existing street network and adding to the 
network. The plat proposes adding nine new streets with three connections into the existing 
Sweetgrass Drive and a connection into Sweetgrass 5th Filings subdivision. The proposed street 
network is in compliance with the network proposed by the Sweetgrass development. 
 
With the development continuing to move further south, a second major connection to the west 
will be required with future subdivisions. Currently all traffic is being funneled to Sweetgrass 
Drive and up to College Drive. An Emergency secondary access has been provided to the west 
that will be built with this development.   
 
Maximum and minimum block dimensions are established by the Unified Development Code. 
Block 17, 19, 21, and 26 within this proposed subdivision do not meet the block standards put 
forth in the code; however, the oversized blocks appear to qualify for exceptions and the overall 
pattern still provides an interconnected system of streets. Staff recommends that block 17, 19, 
and 26 qualify for the Natural Features and Open Space System exception (see exception 1). 
 
Community Facility Fees: 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. The 
required fees for the 39.5 acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$13,825 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$13,825 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$27,650.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee 
$1,100 per acre 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
 
Lot 1, Block 16 is designated as a future Elementary School and Lot 1, Block 25 is designated 
as a future Joint Police and Fire Station. Should these parcels not develop as such, additional 
Land Acquisition and Infrastructure fees will be required at time of development. 
 
 
Open Space: 
Open Space parcels are provided on this plat. They are platted as Tracks AA through II and 
labeled as Private Trails/ Open Space. These tracks are part of Sweetgrass PUD trail system. 
These open space parcels make up 21.06-acres of the proposed plat.  
  
Required Improvements: 
This division can provide all required utilities to the properties within this development.  
 
The BOPU provided the following as a general comment: 
Per the BOPU’s Infrastructure Policies & Design Criteria, UTILITY EXTENSIONS AND 
CONNECTIONS  III.A.1.  Extensions Required Across the Property. As per sections 1.16.040, 
1.16.050 and 1.16.060 of Chapter 1.16 of the CMC, the property owner/developer shall be 

UDC-21-00094  
 
 
July 6, 2021 
required to extend the water and sanitary sewer utilities to the farthest point (or points) of the 
property being developed. All water mains must be a looped system. These water and sewer 
utility extensions shall be to a point (or points) that are logical for projected future connections 
by future development projects, as determined by the BOPU and the property 
owner/developer. With the approval of this Preliminary and the Final Plat, the BOPU does not 
give their final approval of the Water and Sanitary Sewer layout shown on the Preliminary Plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. The Sweetgrass PUD calls this portion of the 
2,000 plus acre Sweetgrass site, part of phase 1 through 3 of 10.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A preliminary Drainage Report was submitted and reviewed. It was found that the plan meets 
the intent of the preliminary drainage report requirements contained in Article 3 of the 
Cheyenne UDC.   
 
Traffic: 
A Traffic Report was submitted with the final Sweetgrass PUD. The proposed platting action was 
reviewed and found to be within the scope of the original Traffic Report.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat is one of the MDR zones of the PUD. The MDR is intended to provide for 
residential development allowing for a variety of single-family detached and attached dwelling 
units, located nearby to schools and commercial centers. An extensive open space network 
should be provided with within each MDR parcel. To promote greater diversity, lower density 
development, such as that permitted within LDR districts, is permitted within MDR districts. All 
lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 

UDC-21-00094  
 
 
July 6, 2021 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The PUD requires common lots to be owned, developed, improved, managed, and/or 
maintained by homeowners’ associations, architectural review board(s), community 
development districts, and/or similar entities (PUD Section 1, Subsection G). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat is in conformance with the design and layout anticipated for this parcel by the 
Sweetgrass PUD. Adjacent Sweetgrass developments were designed and laid out with this 
development as an integral part of the broader development pattern.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
Public Safety: 
This development represents a continued expansion and an increase in public safety service in 
the City.   Recent preliminary plats have been required to contribute a monetary amount on a 
per unit basis for public safety fees to be placed in the Development Impact Fund earmarked for 
the purpose of enhancing the City’s fire rescue infrastructure. Staff has recommended a 
(Condition 1.c) that the developer enter into a development agreement for this public safety 
prior to final plat approval.  This dollar amount utilized in $756 per unit based on January 2021 
costs.   The City will utilize that base fee and adjust for inflation utilizing the  construction cost 
index for twenty (20) cities, as issued by the current Engineering News Record which is similarly 
used for calculating infrastructure reimbursements pursuant to Cheyenne Municipal Code 
subsection 1.16.070.b at the time the plat is scheduled for the Governing Body.  Based on 
January 2021 numbers, the fee for 149 units is $112,644.  When adjusted for 3.78% inflation 
through June 2021, the estimated total public safety fees is $127,279.94 ($112,644 * 03.78%).  
This number will be finalized in the agreement for the time the plat is scheduled for Governing 
Body consideration. 
 
The Fire Department also asks that the applicant provide a temporary fire access to the west 
with their future Engineering Plan Review submittal and subdivision construction. The applicant 
has provided a document showing the proposed secondary access. 
 
The application complies with conditions for this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 

UDC-21-00094  
 
 
July 6, 2021 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff has recommended 
conditions to the plat. These conditions are summarized in the staff recommendation. While this 
condition does not pose a significant engineering or planning issue, it should be addressed prior 
to the Governing Body’s consideration of the Final Plat.  
 
The application complies with conditions for this review criterion. 
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Open Space parcel BB and GG are not labeled on the Plat. (resolved by map revisions) 
2. A statement in the traffic memo should be provided stating that this development will 
not require a signal to be installed at the intersection of Sweetgrass and College 
because the proposed developments traffic generation does not yet warrant one. 
(resolved by revisions) 
3. Wallick Road should be labeled as E Nation Road. (resolved by map revisions) 
4. Sirocco Drive appears to be acting as a collector based on the amount of lots feeding 
the roadway network. Engineering would like to discuss this further. (resolved by map 
revisions)  
5. Curve tables should be shown on the preliminary plat. (resolved by map revisions) 
6. Water and Sewer should be shown on the preliminary plat. (resolved by map revisions) 

UDC-21-00094  
 
 
July 6, 2021 
7. Secondary access road for Emergency response must meet IFC Appendix D 
requirements. An exhibit should be show with the preliminary plat showing the 
location, grade, and how it meets IFC requirements needs to be provided. (provided) 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
This application meets all review criteria as filed with conditions. 
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body (Staff Recommendation). 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add conditions, or 
decide contrary to staff’s recommendation.    In the event conditions are modified, the conditions shall relate 
to applicable review criteria.  In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or 
modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for 
approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the Preliminary Plat subject to the following conditions: 
1. Enter into a development agreement for public safety fees. Public safety fees shall be based on a 
January 2021 fee of $756 per unit and shall be adjusted for inflation utilizing the construction 
cost index for twenty (20) cities, as issued by the current Engineering News Record to the date 
of final plat filing. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat 
6. Secondary Access
